  • Mailbox Unit

Mailbox Unit

1. Overview

This document specifies the Mailbox Unit hardware IP functionality. The Mailbox Unit provides FIFO-based bidirectional communication channels for inter-processor or inter-chiplet communication using AXI4-Lite interfaces. It enables efficient data exchange between processing elements through dedicated hardware channels with minimal software overhead.

2. Description and Features

The Mailbox Unit enables secure, FIFO-based, bidirectional communication between processing elements or chiplets in multicore systems. Each configurable mailbox instance consists of paired inbound and outbound channels, allowing simultaneous data transfer with a 64-bit data path. Multiple independent mailbox pairs are supported, each equipped with dedicated control registers, status monitoring, and programmable interrupt thresholds.

The architecture uses standard AXI4-Lite interfaces for integration and provides robust error detection, reporting, and software-controllable FIFO flushing. Channel depth is adjustable per instance to suit system needs, while hardware minimizes software overhead for efficient, low-latency data exchange.

Figure 1. SMC mailbox wrapper and interrupt routing

SMC mailbox wrapper and interrupt routing shows the SMC instance of the mailbox, u_smc_axil_mailbox, and its interrupt connections. The SMC configures 32 mailboxes of depth 2, with 32-bit address and 64-bit data. The port comes from the mailbox target of smc_internal_axi_lite_xbar, and the unit runs on a copy of the SMC clock that u_mailbox_cg gates while the port is idle. The demux u_mailbox_demux, a PULP axi_lite_demux, selects one of 64 ports from address bits [16:11] above 0xC001_8000. Each mailbox, gen_mailbox[m].u_axi_lite_mailbox, is a PULP axi_lite_mailbox; the figure puts the source project in parentheses. Mailbox m places its outbound port at 0xC001_8000 + m × 0x1000 and its inbound port 0x800 above it.

Within a mailbox, the arrows between the ports show the direction of data: WRITE_DATA in one port pushes the FIFO that READ_DATA in the other port pops. The 32 inbound-port interrupts are SMC CPU interrupts 288 to 319. The 32 outbound-port interrupts leave the SMC on smc_ext_mailbox_interrupts_o, which the SMU passes out of its boundary.

The SEP holds a second instance of this unit with eight mailboxes, described in the SEP fabric chapter. The outbound interrupts of that instance reach the SMC as peripheral interrupts 0 to 7, SMC CPU interrupts 256 to 263.

3. Architecture

The Mailbox IP provides inter-processor and inter-chiplet communication through a register-based interface. A single AXI4-Lite slave port is demuxed into one register aperture per mailbox direction, and each mailbox pair owns the two FIFOs that carry data between its two apertures.

3.1. Configuration Parameters

The parameters and ports of axi_lite_mailbox_unit are listed in axi_lite_mailbox_unit.

3.2. Address Space

The architecture supports up to 64 mailbox instances (32 bidirectional pairs):

  • Total Space: 2 × MAILBOX_SIZE × NUM_MAILBOXES, decoded from a port that is ADDR_WIDTH bits wide

  • Data Width: 64 bits

  • Per-Aperture Space: 0x800 bytes (2KB)

  • Decoding: Address bits above $clog2(MAILBOX_SIZE) select one of 2 × NUM_MAILBOXES apertures

The 64-bit data width handles addresses, data payloads and status information without multi-word transfers. The 2KB allocation per aperture leaves the ten registers sparsely mapped; the SMC and SEP memory maps state how the offsets above them answer.

3.3. Internal Structure

The unit is an axi_lite_demux in front of NUM_MAILBOXES mailbox instances. Each mailbox instance has two AXI4-Lite slave ports, one per aperture, and holds two message FIFOs of MAILBOX_DEPTH entries each. The FIFOs are cross-coupled between the two ports: the push side of one port is the pop side of the other. The FIFOs are internal to the IP; there is no external FIFO interface.

3.4. Transaction Processing

AXI4-Lite transactions follow this flow:

  1. Aperture Decode: The demux selects an aperture from the address bits above $clog2(MAILBOX_SIZE), relative to MAILBOX_BASE_ADDR

  2. Register Decode: The mailbox slave port decodes the offset within the aperture into one of the ten registers

  3. FIFO Access: WRITE_DATA pushes the port’s write FIFO, READ_DATA pops the port’s read FIFO; full and empty conditions set the error flags described in the Error Handling table

  4. Response: The B and R responses are emitted through spill registers, giving one cycle of latency and keeping the response path free of combinational paths from the request

3.5. Bidirectional Communication

Each mailbox pair provides two register apertures, conventionally used as an outbound and an inbound direction. The two are not independent storage: they share the same pair of FIFOs, so WRITE_DATA in one aperture is what READ_DATA in the other aperture returns. Both apertures hang off the same AXI4-Lite slave port, so a local agent can drive either end; the direction naming reflects intended use, not an access restriction.

Separate apertures give each direction its own thresholds, status, error flags and interrupt output.

3.6. Clock and Reset

The mailbox uses a single clock domain for integration reliability.

No internal clock domain crossings eliminates CDC complexity. Single clock domain simplifies timing analysis and integration verification.

3.7. Interrupt Architecture

The mailbox provides independent interrupt outputs for each mailbox instance:

  • inbound_interrupt_o[NUM_MAILBOXES-1:0]: Inbound communication events

  • outbound_interrupt_o[NUM_MAILBOXES-1:0]: Outbound communication events

Separate inbound/outbound interrupts reflect different processing requirements. Inbound interrupts indicate incoming commands requiring immediate processing; outbound interrupts indicate successful transmission or buffer availability.

Mailbox interrupt sources reports the interrupt sources.

Table 1. Mailbox interrupt sources
Source Description

Write Threshold

FIFO fill level exceeds configured threshold (proactive buffer management)

Read Threshold

FIFO data available for efficient retrieval scheduling

Access Error

Write to full FIFO or read from empty FIFO; both set the same error interrupt bit (fault detection)

Each interrupt output aggregates these sources and is level-triggered and active high. Threshold-based interrupts reduce polling overhead; error interrupts enable fault recovery and diagnostic analysis.

4. Interface

The Mailbox Unit exposes a single struct-based AXI4-Lite slave port. All mailbox apertures are decoded from this one port by an internal demux.

4.1. AXI4-Lite Interface

input  axi_req_t  mailbox_axi_req_i,
output axi_resp_t mailbox_axi_resp_o

The request and response struct types are supplied by the integrator through the axi_req_t/axi_resp_t parameters, together with the per-channel types aw_chan_t, w_chan_t, b_chan_t, ar_chan_t and r_chan_t.

Mailbox Interface Characteristics summarises the AXI4-Lite register interface characteristics.

Table 2. Mailbox Interface Characteristics
Parameter Value

Address Width

ADDR_WIDTH parameter

Data Width

DATA_WIDTH parameter

Strobe Width

DATA_WIDTH/8

Total Address Space

2 × MAILBOX_SIZE × NUM_MAILBOXES

Protocol

Standard AXI4-Lite with separate read/write channels

4.2. Interrupt Outputs

output logic [NUM_MAILBOXES-1:0] inbound_interrupt_o,
output logic [NUM_MAILBOXES-1:0] outbound_interrupt_o

Mailbox Interrupt Characteristics describes the triggering and configuration of mailbox interrupts.

Table 3. Mailbox Interrupt Characteristics
Parameter Value

Type

Level-triggered (the unit ties the underlying IrqEdgeTrig to 0)

Polarity

Active high

Sources

Write threshold, read threshold, and a single shared error bit (read from empty or write to full)

4.3. Control Signals

input logic clk_i,       // System clock
input logic rst_ni,      // Active-low asynchronous reset
input logic test_en_i    // Test enable for DFT

4.4. Timing Characteristics

Mailbox timing characteristics summarises latency, throughput, and outstanding-transaction capacity.

Table 4. Mailbox timing characteristics
Parameter Value

Latency

One clock cycle; the R and B responses pass through spill registers

Throughput

One 64-bit transfer per clock cycle

Outstanding Transactions

MAX_TRANS per demux port (default: 32)

4.5. Error Handling

Mailbox error handling describes how empty reads, full writes, and interrupt conditions are handled.

Table 5. Mailbox error handling
Error Condition Behavior

Read from empty FIFO (READ_DATA)

Sets read_error in ERROR_FLAGS and the error bit in IRQS; returns 0xFEEDDEAD with SLVERR

Write to full FIFO (WRITE_DATA)

Sets write_error in ERROR_FLAGS and the error bit in IRQS; discards the data and responds SLVERR

Read from write-only WRITE_DATA

Returns 0xFEEDC0DE with OKAY; no FIFO access and no error flag

Write to a read-only register (READ_DATA, STATUS, ERROR_FLAGS, IRQP)

Write is ignored, responds SLVERR

Access to an unmapped offset inside an aperture (0x50 and above)

Stated in the SMC and SEP memory maps

Read of ERROR_FLAGS

Both flags clear on read; an error raised in the same cycle as the clearing read is retained

Interrupt Generation

Triggered if enabled via IRQEN register

4.6. Integration Requirements

Mailbox integration requirements lists the address-space and system-integration requirements.

Table 6. Mailbox integration requirements
Requirement Description

Address Space

MAILBOX_SIZE (2KB) per aperture, 4KB per mailbox pair; Total = 4KB × NUM_MAILBOXES

Software

Poll STATUS or use interrupts for data availability; configure WIRQT/RIRQT thresholds; check ERROR register

Hardware

Connect interrupts to system controller; proper reset sequencing; single clock domain (no CDC required)

Address Space Characteristics:

  • Per-Aperture Size: MAILBOX_SIZE, 2KB (0x800)

  • Total Address Space: 0x20000 bytes for 32 pairs

  • Decoded Address Span: 17 bits for 32 pairs; the slave port itself is ADDR_WIDTH bits wide (32 in the SMC and SEP integrations)

  • Maximum Mailboxes: 32 pairs (64 apertures)

The two apertures of a pair are the two ends of the same pair of FIFOs: WRITE_DATA in the outbound aperture pushes the FIFO that READ_DATA in the paired inbound aperture pops, and vice versa. Both ends are reachable from the same AXI4-Lite slave port.

5.2. Per-Mailbox Register Map

Within each 2KB mailbox aperture, registers are organized as follows. Offsets are spaced by DATA_WIDTH/8, so they are 8 bytes apart in the 64-bit configurations used today. The generated detailed register map below is authoritative for offsets, widths, access, and field descriptions. The CTRL flush bits are pulses rather than stored state, so reads return zero with an OKAY response.

The SMC and SEP memory maps state how an offset above the registers answers.

5.3. Address Examples

For mailbox pair N, the outbound aperture begins at BASE + N * 0x1000; the inbound aperture begins 0x800 bytes later. A register address is the aperture base plus the generated register offset. For example, in pair zero, READ_DATA is at BASE + 0x8 outbound and BASE + 0x808 inbound.

5.4. Detailed Register Map

AXI-Lite Mailbox Address Map

Register interface for a single AXI-Lite mailbox: data FIFOs, level and status flags, and interrupt controls.

Register List:

AddressNameAccessDescription
0x0WRITE_DATAW-
0x8READ_DATAR-
0x10STATUSR-
0x18ERROR_FLAGSR-
0x20WIRQTRW-
0x28RIRQTRW-
0x30IRQSRWThis register is used to read and clear interrupt requests, regardless of IRQEN register and whether the interrupt is enabled or disabled.
0x38IRQENRWThis register is used to enable and disable which interrupts are sent to the CPU.
0x40IRQPRThis register is used to read the current status of the interrupts sent to the CPU.
0x48CTRLW-

Register Details:

WRITE_DATA

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:0write_dataW WMOD0x0Write data to mailbox fifo

READ_DATA

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:0read_dataR RMOD-Read data from mailbox fifo

STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:4Reserved--Reserved
3read_level_above_threshR0x0If set, read fifo level is higher than threshold set in RIRQT
2write_level_above_threshR0x0If set, write fifo level is higher than threshold set in WIRQT
1fullR0x00: Space is available to write, 1: Space is not available to write
0emptyR0x10: Data is available to read, 1: Data is not available to read

ERROR_FLAGS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:2Reserved--Reserved
1write_errorR RC0x0Attempted write to a full mailbox
0read_errorR RC0x0Attempted read from an empty mailbox

WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

RIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0rirqtRW0x0When the fill pointer of the FIFO connected to the R channel exceeds this value, a read threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the read FIFO is full.

5.5. Subsystem Wrapper Maps

The wrapper maps describe the generated SMC and SEP mailbox compositions. Their offsets are relative to the corresponding wrapper aperture.

SMC AXI-Lite Mailbox Wrapper Address Map

SMC-side wrapper instantiating the AXI-Lite mailbox pairs.
Each outbound_mailbox_N / inbound_mailbox_N pair is the two apertures of one
bidirectional mailbox and shares a single pair of FIFOs: data written through
WRITE_DATA in one aperture is what READ_DATA in the other aperture returns.
The outbound/inbound naming reflects intended use, not an access restriction.

Register List:

AddressNameAccessDescription
0x0outbound_mailbox_0.WRITE_DATAW-
0x8outbound_mailbox_0.READ_DATAR-
0x10outbound_mailbox_0.STATUSR-

0x1F848inbound_mailbox_31.CTRLW-

Register Details:

outbound_mailbox_0.WRITE_DATA

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:0write_dataW WMOD0x0Write data to mailbox fifo

outbound_mailbox_0.READ_DATA

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:0read_dataR RMOD-Read data from mailbox fifo

outbound_mailbox_0.STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:4Reserved--Reserved
3read_level_above_threshR0x0If set, read fifo level is higher than threshold set in RIRQT
2write_level_above_threshR0x0If set, write fifo level is higher than threshold set in WIRQT
1fullR0x00: Space is available to write, 1: Space is not available to write
0emptyR0x10: Data is available to read, 1: Data is not available to read

outbound_mailbox_0.ERROR_FLAGS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:2Reserved--Reserved
1write_errorR RC0x0Attempted write to a full mailbox
0read_errorR RC0x0Attempted read from an empty mailbox

outbound_mailbox_0.WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

outbound_mailbox_0.RIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0rirqtRW0x0When the fill pointer of the FIFO connected to the R channel exceeds this value, a read threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the read FIFO is full.

outbound_mailbox_0.IRQS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:3Reserved--Reserved
2eirqRW1C0x0On read:
[0]: No interrupt request
[1]: Error on mailbox access
On write:
[0]: No acknowledge
[1]: Acknowledge and clear interrupt request
1rtirqRW1C0x0On read:
[0]: No interrupt request
[1]: Usage level threshold in read mailbox exceeded
On write:
[0]: No acknowledge
[1]: Acknowledge and clear interrupt request
0wtirqRW1C0x0On read:
[0]: No interrupt request
[1]: Usage level threshold in write mailbox exceeded
On write:
[0]: No acknowledge
[1]: Acknowledge and clear interrupt request

outbound_mailbox_0.IRQEN

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:3Reserved--Reserved
2eirqRW0x0[0]: Error IRQ disabled
[1]: Error IRQ enabled
1rtirqRW0x0[0]: Read threshold IRQ disabled
[1]: Read threshold IRQ enabled
0wtirqRW0x0[0]: Write threshold IRQ disabled
[1]: Write threshold IRQ enabled

outbound_mailbox_0.IRQP

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:3Reserved--Reserved
2eirqR0x0[0]: No error IRQ pending
[1]: Error IRQ pending
1rtirqR0x0[0]: No read threshold IRQ pending
[1]: Read threshold IRQ pending
0wtirqR0x0[0]: No write threshold IRQ pending
[1]: Write threshold IRQ pending

outbound_mailbox_0.CTRL

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription

inbound_mailbox_8.WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

inbound_mailbox_12.WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

SEP AXI-Lite Mailbox Wrapper Address Map

SEP-side wrapper instantiating the AXI-Lite mailbox pairs.
Each outbound_mailbox_N / inbound_mailbox_N pair is the two apertures of one
bidirectional mailbox and shares a single pair of FIFOs: data written through
WRITE_DATA in one aperture is what READ_DATA in the other aperture returns.
The outbound/inbound naming reflects intended use, not an access restriction.

Register List:

AddressNameAccessDescription
0x0outbound_mailbox_0.WRITE_DATAW-
0x8outbound_mailbox_0.READ_DATAR-
0x10outbound_mailbox_0.STATUSR-
0x18outbound_mailbox_0.ERROR_FLAGSR-
0x20outbound_mailbox_0.WIRQTRW-
0x28outbound_mailbox_0.RIRQTRW-
0x30outbound_mailbox_0.IRQSRWThis register is used to read and clear interrupt requests, regardless of IRQEN register and whether the interrupt is enabled or disabled.
0x38outbound_mailbox_0.IRQENRWThis register is used to enable and disable which interrupts are sent to the CPU.
0x40outbound_mailbox_0.IRQPRThis register is used to read the current status of the interrupts sent to the CPU.
0x48outbound_mailbox_0.CTRLW-

0x808inbound_mailbox_0.READ_DATAR-
0x810inbound_mailbox_0.STATUSR-
0x818inbound_mailbox_0.ERROR_FLAGSR-
0x820inbound_mailbox_0.WIRQTRW-
0x828inbound_mailbox_0.RIRQTRW-
0x830inbound_mailbox_0.IRQSRWThis register is used to read and clear interrupt requests, regardless of IRQEN register and whether the interrupt is enabled or disabled.
0x838inbound_mailbox_0.IRQENRWThis register is used to enable and disable which interrupts are sent to the CPU.
0x840inbound_mailbox_0.IRQPRThis register is used to read the current status of the interrupts sent to the CPU.
0x848inbound_mailbox_0.CTRLW-

outbound_mailbox_0.STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:4Reserved--Reserved
3read_level_above_threshR0x0If set, read fifo level is higher than threshold set in RIRQT
2write_level_above_threshR0x0If set, write fifo level is higher than threshold set in WIRQT
1fullR0x00: Space is available to write, 1: Space is not available to write
0emptyR0x10: Data is available to read, 1: Data is not available to read

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription

0read_errorR RC0x0Attempted read from an empty mailbox

outbound_mailbox_0.WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

outbound_mailbox_0.RIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0rirqtRW0x0When the fill pointer of the FIFO connected to the R channel exceeds this value, a read threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the read FIFO is full.

63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0rirqtRW0x0When the fill pointer of the FIFO connected to the R channel exceeds this value, a read threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the read FIFO is full.
